- Upgrade an existing project to use `"@elizaos/core": "^1.0.7"` or start a fresh one using this version
- Create a new character file with character settings and load that as the agent in the `index.ts`
- Start the server
- Open `localhost` to view the chat/dev environment and check if custom character is there -- we only saw Eliza and not our agent

Code snippets for characters:

`herbert.ts` snippet:

```typescript

    ...(process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY ? ['@elizaos/plugin-anthropic'] : []),
    ...(process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY ? ['@elizaos/plugin-openai'] : []),

```

`index.ts`:
```typescript

};

  agents: [herbertAgent],
};

**Expected behavior**

- Expected that Eliza would find the new character (Herbert) and register it as an agent (previous behavior in existing project)
- We didn't change any config in the existing project but after upgrading versions we could no longer find/interact with Herbert

**Additional context**

- Tested this with both a newly scaffolded project as well as in an existing project. Our existing project had our custom agent/character loading but I then upgraded to `1.0.7` and Eliza is the only registered agent. Seems to possibly be using Eliza as a fallback and ignoring our custom character files, even though they're being detected when the server starts.

8:T958,   Bug Report: thanks to claude code....

  Issue Summary

  LOG_LEVEL set in .env files is ignored, but works when
  set via command line environment variables.

  Root Cause

  Timing/Order of Operations Problem: The core logger is
  initialized during module import (before .env loading)
  and never rechecks environment variables.

  Technical Details

  Problem Code Locations:

  1. Early Logger Initialization
  (packages/core/src/logger.ts):
  // Lines 148-152 - Logger configured at module import 
  time
  const isDebugMode = (process?.env?.LOG_LEVEL ||
  '').toLowerCase() === 'debug';
  const effectiveLogLevel = isDebugMode ? 'debug' :
  process?.env?.DEFAULT_LOG_LEVEL || 'info';

  2. Late .env Loading
  (packages/cli/src/commands/start.ts):
  // Lines 334-380 - .env loaded much later in start 
  command
  const loadEnvConfig = () => {
    // ... .env file processing happens here
  }

  Problematic Sequence:

  1. Node.js starts → CLI imports modules
  2. logger.ts imported → reads process.env.LOG_LEVEL 
  (empty) → configures 'info' level
  3. Much later: start command executes
  4. Much later: .env file loaded → LOG_LEVEL set in
  process.env
  5. Logger already configured, never rechecks LOG_LEVEL

  Why Command Line Works:

  Command line env vars are set before Node.js starts, so
  they're available during early logger initialization.

  Evidence

  - ✅ Command line: LOG_LEVEL=debug elizaos start → Debug
   logs appear
  - ❌ .env file: LOG_LEVEL=debug → Debug logs don't
  appear
  - ❌ Runtime check: Logger configuration is static after
   module import

  Code Snippets

  Logger reads LOG_LEVEL too early:
  // packages/core/src/logger.ts:151-152
  const isDebugMode = (process?.env?.LOG_LEVEL ||
  '').toLowerCase() === 'debug';
  const effectiveLogLevel = isDebugMode ? 'debug' :
  process?.env?.DEFAULT_LOG_LEVEL || 'info';

  But .env loading happens later:
  // packages/cli/src/commands/start.ts:334-380  
  const loadEnvConfig = () => {
    // Environment loading happens in start command, after
   logger init
  }

  Proposed Fix

  Make logger initialization lazy/dynamic - defer
  LOG_LEVEL reading until after .env files are loaded, or
  add a mechanism to reconfigure logger when environment
  changes.

  This is a clear architectural issue where static module
  initialization happens before dynamic environment
  loading.9:T49f,### Version
1.0.3

### Description
`processKnowledgeAction` uses the following to generate an unique uuid for new memories:
`clientDocumentId: createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now(), "user-knowledge"),`

However, createUniqueUuid does the following:
```
export const createUniqueUuid = (runtime, baseUserId: UUID | string): UUID => {
  // If the base user ID is the agent ID, return it directly
  if (baseUserId === runtime.agentId) {
    return runtime.agentId;
  }

  // Use a deterministic approach to generate a new UUID based on both IDs
  // This creates a unique ID for each user+agent combination while still being deterministic
  const combinedString = `${baseUserId}:${runtime.agentId}`;

  // Create a namespace UUID (version 5) from the combined string
  return stringToUuid(combinedString);
};
```

Meaning that `createUniqueUuid` will always return: `stringToUuid("user-knowledge:undefined")` ie: `4e394ea6-af35-0010-8047-94bccb8b8e5b`

### Expected Result
The generated ID should be unique, the easiest approach would be to us `stringToUuid` directly.

If possible, I would love to contribute. I can fix this issue, and others I've discovered.

Comprehensive refactoring of ALL major CLI commands from monolithic architecture to modular structure for improved maintainability and scalability. This PR systematically transforms large single-file implementations into well-organized, reusable modules following a consistent architectural pattern.



## Architecture Pattern

Every command now follows this consistent modular structure:

```
commands/[command]/
├── index.ts          # Command registration & main entry point
├── types.ts          # TypeScript interfaces & type definitions  
├── actions/          # Core business logic & operations
│   ├── [feature].ts  # Specific action implementations
│   └── index.ts      # Action exports
└── utils/           # Helper functions & utilities
    ├── [utility].ts  # Specific utility implementations
    └── index.ts      # Utility exports
```

## Refactoring Strategy

### **Complete Modularization**
- **87 files** created/modified in commands directory
- **10 major commands** fully refactored
- **Consistent patterns** across all modules
- **Zero breaking changes** - all imports continue to work

- **Shared**: Common functionality across commands

- Consistent patterns across ALL commands

## Migration Details
### Before (prompts library):
```typescript
const response = await prompts({
  type: 'text', 
  name: 'value',
  message: 'Enter value:'
});
```

### After (@clack/prompts):
```typescript
const value = await clack.text({
  message: 'Enter value:',
  validate: (input) => input ? undefined : 'Value required'
});

if (clack.isCancel(value)) {
  clack.cancel('Operation cancelled.');
  process.exit(0);
}
```10:T87e,## Overview

In testing passing in calldata, we noticed that the `data` field was always `null` even though we were including it. When adding the changes to the `transferTemplate` to look for data, and also including it in the XML, we no longer saw it coming through as `null`. 

The template now properly extracts and passes through any provided data/calldata, and the extended type guard in `transfer.ts` addresses an edge case where data was sometimes sent as `'null'` which didn't trigger the initial default to `0x`.

## Changes

### Template Updates
Added support for data/calldata in the transfer template by adding:
- Instruction to extract "Additional data/calldata (if any is included)"
- XML response field `<data>string | null</data>`

Previously,  the `TransferParams` interface supported an optional data parameter but it seemed that not being included in the template meant it was always `null`, even when provided in the request.

These changes enable proper handling of transaction data in transfers while protecting against LLM response variations. The template now properly extracts and passes through any provided data/calldata, and the code safely normalizes edge cases to ensure valid transaction data.

The `bun.lock` change is from a fresh install of the repo.

**Note**: I tested this on version `1.0.6` but haven't been able to run `1.0.7` due to the character issue I filed on the main repo. Wanted to put up the PR in the meantime! Thank you -- greatly appreciate all the work ya'll are doing to transition!

### Type Guard Change to Handle Edge Case

Added additional defensive handling in `TransferAction` for LLM response edge cases:
```typescript
// Normalize empty or invalid data field to '0x'
if (!params.data || params.data === '0x') {
  params.data = '0x';
} else {
  // Handle case where template returns 'null' string
  params.data = (params.data as string) === 'null' ? '0x' : params.data;
}
```
This handles cases where the LLM returns 'null' as a string instead of a null value, ensuring the transaction data is always properly formatted.

 0 fail

**Bug fixes** (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

Users experienced poor UX where deleted messages remained visible in the chat interface until manually refreshing the page. The DELETE API worked correctly but the UI wasn't receiving real-time updates via SocketIO events.

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

- ✅ Sentry error tracking intact16:Tfaa,## Problem

The ElizaOS CLI had scattered and inconsistent directory detection logic throughout the codebase:

1. **Missing monorepo structure detection** - No proper classification for subdirectories within the ElizaOS monorepo
2. **Scattered detection functions** - Multiple functions like `isMonorepoContext()` duplicated detection logic across different files
3. **Infrastructure packages misclassified** - CLI, client, and other monorepo packages were incorrectly detected as `elizaos-project` instead of infrastructure components
4. **Inconsistent detection patterns** - Different commands used different approaches to determine directory types
5. **Over-aggressive heuristics** - Dependency-based detection caused false positives for infrastructure packages within the monorepo

## Solution

Implemented a centralized, hierarchical directory detection system with a new `elizaos-subdir` type:

### New Directory Type

- Added `elizaos-subdir` type for subdirectories within the ElizaOS monorepo that aren't projects or plugins

### Simplified Logic

- **Outside monorepo**: Check if project/plugin → if not, classify as `non-elizaos-dir`
- **Inside monorepo**: Check if project/plugin → if not, classify as `elizaos-subdir`

### Conservative Detection

- Made `isElizaOSProject()` more conservative inside monorepos by only using explicit indicators
- Disabled heuristic-based detection (dependency patterns, file structure analysis) within monorepos
- Consolidated duplicate detection functions

## Details

### Core Changes

**Directory Detection (`packages/cli/src/utils/directory-detection.ts`)**

- Added `elizaos-subdir` to `DirectoryInfo` type union
- Simplified main detection logic to use clear inside/outside monorepo branching
- Made `isElizaOSProject()` accept `monorepoRoot` parameter for context-aware detection
- Disabled heuristic checks inside monorepos to prevent infrastructure package misclassification

**CLI Commands**

- All CLI commands now use the standardized `detectDirectoryType()` function
- Removed deprecated `isMonorepoContext()` function usage

**Dependencies**

- Eliminated duplicate logic in `get-package-info.ts`
- Added `monorepoRoot` property to `DirectoryInfo` for consistent access
- Updated registry logic to use consolidated detection

### Test Results

Before fix:

```bash
# CLI directory incorrectly detected as project
✅ Directory Type: elizaos-project  # ❌ Wrong

# Client directory correctly detected
✅ Directory Type: elizaos-subdir   # ✅ Correct
```

After fix (expected):

```bash
# Both CLI and client correctly detected as subdirs
✅ Directory Type: elizaos-subdir   # ✅ Correct for both
```

### Directory Type Hierarchy

1. **elizaos-monorepo** - Root of ElizaOS monorepo
2. **elizaos-project** - User projects with agents/characters
3. **elizaos-plugin** - ElizaOS plugins
4. **elizaos-subdir** - Infrastructure packages within monorepo (CLI, client, core, etc.)
5. **non-elizaos-dir** - Regular directories outside ElizaOS context

These changes collectively enhance the observability and usability of agent actions and their associated prompts, improving both runtime tracking and client-side display.1a:T160b,# Fix CLI Database and Config File Creation in Wrong Directories

## Problem

The ElizaOS CLI commands (`elizaos start` and `elizaos dev`) were creating database (`.elizadb`) and configuration (`.eliza`) files in incorrect locations, causing several issues:

1. **Monorepo Root Pollution**: When running from projects/plugins inside the monorepo, files were created in the monorepo root instead of the current working directory
2. **Subdirectory Execution Issues**: Running commands from subdirectories (e.g., `packages/cli`) would create files in the monorepo root, not the intended location
3. **Poor User Experience**: No clear guidance when users ran commands from inappropriate directories
4. **Inconsistent Behavior**: Different directory contexts resulted in unpredictable file placement

## Solution

Implemented comprehensive directory validation and centralized error handling:

### 1. **Centralized Directory Validation**

- Added `handleDirectoryContextErrors()` function for consistent validation across commands
- Integrated directory type detection into both `start` and `dev` commands
- Ensured database and config files are always created in the current working directory

### 2. **Enhanced User Guidance**

- **Fatal Errors**: Clear error messages for invalid directory contexts (e.g., running from subdirectories)
- **Warnings**: Styled warnings for monorepo usage with guidance toward best practices
- **Styled Messaging**: Consistent, color-coded error and warning displays matching existing UI patterns

### 3. **Improved Plugin Detection**

- Refined heuristics to reduce false positives in plugin detection
- Fixed issue where `project-tee-starter` was incorrectly flagged as a plugin

### 4. **Deferred Warning Display**

- Monorepo warnings now appear after agent initialization to avoid interrupting startup
- Uses global flag mechanism to schedule warnings for appropriate timing

## Examples

### Before (Problematic Behavior)

```bash
# Running from monorepo root
cd /path/to/eliza
elizaos start
# ❌ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza

# Running from plugin inside monorepo
cd /path/to/eliza/packages/plugin-bootstrap
elizaos start
# ❌ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza (monorepo root)

# Running from subdirectory
cd /path/to/eliza/packages/cli
elizaos start
# ❌ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza (monorepo root)
```

### After (Fixed Behavior)

```bash
# Running from monorepo root
cd /path/to/eliza
elizaos start
# ✅ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza
# ⚠️  Shows warning: "Running from monorepo root is not best practice"

# Running from plugin inside monorepo
cd /path/to/eliza/packages/plugin-bootstrap
elizaos start
# ✅ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza/packages/plugin-bootstrap
# ⚠️  Shows warning: "Running from monorepo root is not best practice"

# Running from subdirectory
cd /path/to/eliza/packages/cli
elizaos start
# ❌ Shows error: "Must run elizaos start from project or plugin directory"
# 🛑 Exits with helpful guidance to run "elizaos create"
```

### Validation Matrix

All directory types now behave correctly:

| Directory Type                     | START | DEV  | Database/Config Location |
| ---------------------------------- | ----- | ---- | ------------------------ |
| elizaos-project (outside monorepo) | ✅    | ✅   | Current directory        |
| elizaos-plugin (outside monorepo)  | ✅    | ✅   | Current directory        |
| elizaos-project (inside monorepo)  | ✅⚠️  | ✅⚠️ | Current directory        |
| elizaos-plugin (inside monorepo)   | ✅⚠️  | ✅⚠️ | Current directory        |
| elizaos-monorepo                   | ✅⚠️  | ✅⚠️ | Current directory        |
| elizaos-subdir                     | ❌    | ❌   | N/A (exits with error)   |

Legend: ✅ = Works, ⚠️ = Warning shown, ❌ = Error shown and exits

https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge/issues/16

This most likely went unnoticed for three reasons:
1. Eliza still responds postively even if the memory is not stored as 'knowledge'.
2. Because the last few messages are in the context window, both the request to store the memory and the positive response from Eliza are in subsequent queries.
3. The browser client correctly calls `createUniqueUuid` with the agent runtime. 

# Risks
**Low Risk** - Instead of using `createUniqueUuid`, we are using `stringToUuid` in two different places

# Background
## What does this PR fix?
The way we generate unique ids for memories, specifically in `actions.ts`:

```ts
// for documents
clientDocumentId: createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + fileName + Date.now(), fileName),
// ...
// for text
clientDocumentId: createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now(), "user-knowledge"),
```

is not unique at all. for text, the result is always `4e394ea6-af35-0010-8047-94bccb8b8e5b` since `createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now(), "user-knowledge")` always results in `stringToUuid("user-knowledge:undefined")`. 

For documents, the result is `stringToUuid(fileName)` which is only unique if the file name is unique.

## Proposed Fix
To maintain the initial intent of the code, we should use `stringToUuid` for both text and documents using the existing seeds: `runtime.agentId + fileName + Date.now()` and `runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now() + "user-knowledge"`.

# Documentation changes needed?
No.

# Testing
## Where should a reviewer start?
1. Two new tests were added to `__tests__/action.test.ts` to ensure that the unique ids are generated correctly.
2. Existings tests were updated to Mock `Date.now()` to generate predictable ids. 
3. To truly verify knowledge is being stored, the "memories" table needs to be checked.

- Adds automatic lockfile cleanup when falling back to GitHub installations
- Prevents circular dependency issues during npm-to-GitHub fallback scenarios

## Changes
- **New `removeFromBunLock()` function**: Safely removes package entries from bun.lock using `bun remove`
- **New `executeInstallationWithFallback()` function**: Handles npm installation with automatic GitHub fallback and lockfile cleanup
- **Updated `installPlugin()` function**: Uses the new fallback mechanism for registry-based installations

## How it works
1. Attempts npm installation first
2. If npm fails and GitHub fallback is available:
   - Calls `removeFromBunLock()` to clean up any partial lockfile entries
   - Attempts GitHub installation with clean lockfile
3. Prevents circular dependency errors that can occur with stale lockfile entries

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes a fundamental routing issue where plugin routes with URL parameters were returning 404 errors. The agent router was using exact string matching instead of pattern matching, causing any route with parameters (`:id`, `:param`, etc.) to fail.

**Changes:**
- Added `path-to-regexp` import to agent router
- Replaced exact path matching (`path === r.path`) with pattern-based matching using `match()` function
- Properly extract URL parameters into `req.params` for plugin handlers

**Affects all plugins with routes like:**
- `/documents/:knowledgeId` (knowledge plugin)
- `/items/:itemId` (any plugin)
- `/users/:userId/settings` (any plugin)

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Review the changes in `eliza/packages/cli/src/server/api/agent.ts` - specifically the plugin route matching logic

## Detailed testing steps

**Test any plugin with parameterized routes:**
- Test knowledge plugin DELETE `/documents/:knowledgeId`
- Test any plugin GET routes with parameters like `/items/:id`
- Verify parameter extraction works correctly

**Before fix:**
```javascript
// path="/documents/12345", r.path="/documents/:knowledgeId"
if (path === r.path) // false - no match, 404 error
```

**After fix:**
```javascript
// Uses path-to-regexp matching for ANY plugin route
matcher = match("/documents/:knowledgeId")
matched = matcher("/documents/12345") // true - extracts parameters
req.params = { ...req.params, knowledgeId: "12345" }
```

## Executive Summary
This week, the project undertook a significant architectural overhaul of the core framework, culminating in the release of version 1.0.9. These foundational improvements focused on enhancing modularity, stability, and the overall developer experience, paving the way for more advanced features and broader community collaboration.

### Key Strategic Initiatives & Outcomes

**Strengthening the Core Framework for Future Growth**
To support our long-term vision of a modular and scalable AI framework, we invested heavily in reorganizing the foundational code to make the system more robust and easier to build upon.
- The core API was restructured into a more logical, domain-based system, improving maintainability and clarity for developers working with our services in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- We re-architected the database layer to support dynamic table loading and migrations, making our data handling more flexible and scalable in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- Core system types and services were refactored into smaller, more manageable modules, which improves code organization and enhances our agents' ability to search and understand the codebase in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).

**Enhancing Developer Experience and API Usability**
To accelerate development and make our platform more accessible, we delivered major improvements to our tools and documentation.
- A comprehensive Postman collection with over 90 documented REST API endpoints was released, making it significantly easier for developers to understand and integrate with the platform in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- The command-line interface (CLI) was modernized with a more intuitive and user-friendly prompt system, improving the developer workflow in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- Development and CI environments were optimized for faster project creation by implementing Bun's offline mode, reducing setup times for contributors in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).

**Improving Stability and User Interaction**
We focused on resolving critical bugs and polishing the user interface to create a more reliable and engaging experience for both agents and human users.
- Version 1.0.9 was released, incorporating numerous fixes that deliver substantial improvements to platform stability and performance in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- Key bugs in agent communication were resolved, ensuring agents are correctly recognized as channel participants and can interact reliably in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- The chat UI was enhanced with animated markdown rendering and improved styling for code blocks, creating a more polished and readable user experience in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).

## Repository Spotlights

### elizaos/eliza
This week's work focused on a major architectural refactor to improve modularity, developer experience, and overall stability, culminating in the v1.0.9 release.

**Key Enhancements:**
- **Architectural Overhaul**: Completed a major refactoring of the type system ([#4999](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4999), [#5020](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5020)), API routes ([#5010](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5010)), and CLI commands ([#5036](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5036)) into a more modular and maintainable structure.
- **Database Improvements**: Rebuilt the SQL plugin with migration capabilities ([#5092](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5092)) and implemented dynamic loading for database tables ([#5018](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5018)).
- **API and Developer Tooling**: Released a comprehensive Postman collection with 90+ endpoints ([#5047](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5047)) and upgraded the CLI to use `@clack/prompts` for a better user experience ([#5016](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5016)).
- **UI/UX Polish**: Enhanced the chat UI with an animated markdown component ([#5111](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5111)) and improved code block styling ([#5105](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5105)).
- **Real-time Communication**: Implemented real-time message deletion via SocketIO ([#4968](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4968)) and added API endpoints to manage agents across channels ([#5113](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/5113)).

**Notable Resolved Issues:**
- Fixed a critical issue preventing agents from being recognized as channel participants ([#4972](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/4972)).
- Resolved errors related to GPT-4o model access ([#5023](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/5023)) and the inability to load custom agent characters after upgrades ([#5039](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/5039)).

I am trying to get rag working and following docs, but that code is not implemented in 1.0.6.  It appears to be commented as a placeholder in 
 
**To Reproduce**

settings: {
    secrets: {},
    ragKnowledge: true,
  },
  knowledge: [
    {
      directory: 'knowledge/foobar',
      shared: true,
    },
  ],

**Expected behavior**

I expect that it would parse on startup and it was not doing that, as it used to do.  So, I looked into code and found this... summary from claude....

Missing Implementation Locations

  1. AgentRuntime Initialization Missing Knowledge 
  Processing

  File: /packages/core/src/runtime.ts (lines 494-651)
  - The AgentRuntime.initialize() method should process
  character.knowledge but doesn't
  - No call to any knowledge processing function during
  agent startup

  2. TODO Comment Confirms Missing Implementation

  File: /packages/core/src/specs/v1/index.ts (line 50)
  // TODO: Implement the remaining adapters: ... - 
  knowledge / memory
  This is a developer comment explicitly stating that
  knowledge processing is not implemented yet.

  3. Bootstrap Plugin Missing KNOWLEDGE Provider

  File: /packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/index.ts
  - Multiple message examples reference providers: 
  ['KNOWLEDGE'] in character files
  - But the bootstrap plugin doesn't export any KNOWLEDGE 
  provider
  - Provider list is incomplete - missing the knowledge
  provider entirely

  4. RagService Interface Exists But No Implementation

  File: /packages/core/src/runtime.ts (lines 52-61)
  interface RagServiceDelegator extends Service {
    getKnowledge(message: Memory, scope?: { roomId?: UUID;
   worldId?: UUID; entityId?: UUID }):
  Promise<KnowledgeItem[]>;
    _internalAddKnowledge(item: KnowledgeItem, options?:
  any, scope?: any): Promise<void>;
  }
  The interface exists but no actual implementation of
  this service.

  5. Missing Functions

  - processCharacterKnowledge() - Referenced in docs but
  doesn't exist anywhere
  - No knowledge file reading/processing logic
  - No connection between character.knowledge array and
  embedding system

  Developer Comments Confirming This

  The codebase has explicit TODO comments indicating that
  knowledge/memory functionality is intentionally 
  unfinished. The character examples even reference
   a KNOWLEDGE provider that doesn't exist, suggesting
  this was planned but never implemented.

39:Ta13,**Describe the bug**

After upgrading an existing project to use `@elizaos/core` version `1.0.7`, our custom character is no longer registered as an agent. Opening the chat interface/dev environment on `localhost` only shows Eliza as an agent and not our character. This previously worked with `1.0.6`. 

I tested with a newly scaffolded project and set up a character there as well and noticed the same behavior with only Eliza being shown as an agent.

**To Reproduce**

- Upgrade an existing project to use `"@elizaos/core": "^1.0.7"` or start a fresh one using this version
- Create a new character file with character settings and load that as the agent in the `index.ts`
- Start the server
- Open `localhost` to view the chat/dev environment and check if custom character is there -- we only saw Eliza and not our agent

Code snippets for characters:

`herbert.ts` snippet:

```typescript
export const herbert: Character = {
  name: 'Herbert',
  plugins: [
    '@elizaos/plugin-sql',
    ...(process.env.EVM_PRIVATE_KEY ? ['@elizaos/plugin-evm'] : []),
    ...(process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY ? ['@elizaos/plugin-anthropic'] : []),
    ...(process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY ? ['@elizaos/plugin-openai'] : []),
    ...(process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY ? ['@elizaos/plugin-knowledge'] : []),
    ...(!process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY ? ['@elizaos/plugin-local-ai'] : []),
    ...(process.env.DISCORD_API_TOKEN ? ['@elizaos/plugin-discord'] : []),
    ...(process.env.TWITTER_USERNAME ? ['@elizaos/plugin-twitter'] : []),
    ...(process.env.TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN ? ['@elizaos/plugin-telegram'] : []),
    ...(!process.env.IGNORE_BOOTSTRAP ? ['@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap'] : []),
  ],
... (more settings)
}
```

`index.ts`:
```typescript
const herbertAgent: ProjectAgent = {
  character: herbert,
  init: async (runtime: IAgentRuntime) => initCharacter({ runtime }),
};
const project: Project = {
  agents: [herbertAgent],
};
```

**Expected behavior**

- Expected that Eliza would find the new character (Herbert) and register it as an agent (previous behavior in existing project)
- We didn't change any config in the existing project but after upgrading versions we could no longer find/interact with Herbert

**Additional context**

- Tested this with both a newly scaffolded project as well as in an existing project. Our existing project had our custom agent/character loading but I then upgraded to `1.0.7` and Eliza is the only registered agent. Seems to possibly be using Eliza as a fallback and ignoring our custom character files, even though they're being detected when the server starts.
3a:T1545,**Describe the bug**

In using the `plugin-evm` transfer function we expect to see a followup message with either the success or failure of the transaction.

In the plugin's `transfer.ts` callback, we see the following:

https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-evm/blob/6bf9c4c54b9e1a558c7fb092f071f2e374435887/src/actions/transfer.ts#L133

```typescript
 if (callback) {
        callback({
          text: `Successfully transferred ${paramOptions.amount} tokens to ${paramOptions.toAddress}\nTransaction Hash: ${transferResp.hash}`,
          content: {
            success: true,
            hash: transferResp.hash,
            amount: formatEther(transferResp.value),
            recipient: transferResp.to,
            chain: paramOptions.fromChain,
          },
        });
      }
... (other error logging as well)

We would expect to see a followup message with the success (or failure) text from the callback in the `transfer.ts` function.

We also triggered an unsuccessful transaction and did not receive a followup error message which the callback suggests we should see.

Our private key and RPC are configured and work as the transfer is successful.

Based on the callback in `transfer.ts` we expect to see a followup message indicating the success or failure of the transfer call.

Response:

3c:T958,   Bug Report: thanks to claude code....

  Issue Summary

  LOG_LEVEL set in .env files is ignored, but works when
  set via command line environment variables.

  Root Cause

  Timing/Order of Operations Problem: The core logger is
  initialized during module import (before .env loading)
  and never rechecks environment variables.

  Technical Details

  Problem Code Locations:

  1. Early Logger Initialization
  (packages/core/src/logger.ts):
  // Lines 148-152 - Logger configured at module import 
  time
  const isDebugMode = (process?.env?.LOG_LEVEL ||
  '').toLowerCase() === 'debug';
  const effectiveLogLevel = isDebugMode ? 'debug' :
  process?.env?.DEFAULT_LOG_LEVEL || 'info';

  2. Late .env Loading
  (packages/cli/src/commands/start.ts):
  // Lines 334-380 - .env loaded much later in start 
  command
  const loadEnvConfig = () => {
    // ... .env file processing happens here
  }

  Problematic Sequence:

  1. Node.js starts → CLI imports modules
  2. logger.ts imported → reads process.env.LOG_LEVEL 
  (empty) → configures 'info' level
  3. Much later: start command executes
  4. Much later: .env file loaded → LOG_LEVEL set in
  process.env
  5. Logger already configured, never rechecks LOG_LEVEL

  Why Command Line Works:

  Command line env vars are set before Node.js starts, so
  they're available during early logger initialization.

  Evidence

  - ✅ Command line: LOG_LEVEL=debug elizaos start → Debug
   logs appear
  - ❌ .env file: LOG_LEVEL=debug → Debug logs don't
  appear
  - ❌ Runtime check: Logger configuration is static after
   module import

  Code Snippets

  Logger reads LOG_LEVEL too early:
  // packages/core/src/logger.ts:151-152
  const isDebugMode = (process?.env?.LOG_LEVEL ||
  '').toLowerCase() === 'debug';
  const effectiveLogLevel = isDebugMode ? 'debug' :
  process?.env?.DEFAULT_LOG_LEVEL || 'info';

  But .env loading happens later:
  // packages/cli/src/commands/start.ts:334-380  
  const loadEnvConfig = () => {
    // Environment loading happens in start command, after
   logger init
  }

  Proposed Fix

  Make logger initialization lazy/dynamic - defer
  LOG_LEVEL reading until after .env files are loaded, or
  add a mechanism to reconfigure logger when environment
  changes.

  This is a clear architectural issue where static module
  initialization happens before dynamic environment
  loading.3d:T49f,### Version
1.0.3

### Description
`processKnowledgeAction` uses the following to generate an unique uuid for new memories:
`clientDocumentId: createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now(), "user-knowledge"),`

However, createUniqueUuid does the following:
```
export const createUniqueUuid = (runtime, baseUserId: UUID | string): UUID => {
  // If the base user ID is the agent ID, return it directly
  if (baseUserId === runtime.agentId) {
    return runtime.agentId;
  }

  // Use a deterministic approach to generate a new UUID based on both IDs
  // This creates a unique ID for each user+agent combination while still being deterministic
  const combinedString = `${baseUserId}:${runtime.agentId}`;

  // Create a namespace UUID (version 5) from the combined string
  return stringToUuid(combinedString);
};
```

Meaning that `createUniqueUuid` will always return: `stringToUuid("user-knowledge:undefined")` ie: `4e394ea6-af35-0010-8047-94bccb8b8e5b`

### Expected Result
The generated ID should be unique, the easiest approach would be to us `stringToUuid` directly.

If possible, I would love to contribute. I can fix this issue, and others I've discovered.

41:Te38,## Summary

Comprehensive refactoring of ALL major CLI commands from monolithic architecture to modular structure for improved maintainability and scalability. This PR systematically transforms large single-file implementations into well-organized, reusable modules following a consistent architectural pattern.



## Architecture Pattern

Every command now follows this consistent modular structure:

```
commands/[command]/
├── index.ts          # Command registration & main entry point
├── types.ts          # TypeScript interfaces & type definitions  
├── actions/          # Core business logic & operations
│   ├── [feature].ts  # Specific action implementations
│   └── index.ts      # Action exports
└── utils/           # Helper functions & utilities
    ├── [utility].ts  # Specific utility implementations
    └── index.ts      # Utility exports
```

## Refactoring Strategy

### **Complete Modularization**
- **87 files** created/modified in commands directory
- **10 major commands** fully refactored
- **Consistent patterns** across all modules
- **Zero breaking changes** - all imports continue to work

## Key Benefits

### **Separation of Concerns**
- **Actions**: Business logic and core operations
- **Utils**: Validation, display, and utility functions  
- **Types**: TypeScript definitions and schemas
- **Shared**: Common functionality across commands

### **Improved Maintainability**
- Individual files are focused and manageable (20-200 lines vs 300-800+ line monoliths)
- Easier to locate and modify specific functionality
- Clear boundaries between different concerns
- Consistent patterns across ALL commands

### **Enhanced Reusability**
- Shared utilities prevent code duplication
- Modular functions can be imported independently
- Common patterns for server management, file watching, plugin handling
- Centralized error handling and validation

### **Better Testing & Development**
- Individual functions can be unit tested in isolation
- Mocking dependencies is simplified
- Development workflows are now standardized
- Plugin management is consistent across commands

## Backward Compatibility

✅ **Zero breaking changes** - All existing CLI commands, options, and workflows work exactly as before. The modular structure coexists with original interfaces through re-export proxies, ensuring full backward compatibility.

## Technical Highlights

- **Development Workflow**: Enhanced dev command with improved file watching and server management
- **Plugin System**: Comprehensive plugin loading, installation, and dependency resolution
- **Registry Integration**: Complete publishing pipeline for npm and GitHub
- **Environment Management**: Interactive configuration with validation

- **Build Orchestration**: Modular build utilities supporting projects, plugins, and monorepos
- **Error Handling**: Consistent error handling patterns across all commands

This PR implements a comprehensive API domain reorganization and improves the client-side confirmation UX.

### Major API Domain Reorganization 🏗️

**Complete restructuring of the `/packages/cli/src/server/api/` directory:**

- **Deleted monolithic files**: Removed large single-file modules (`agent.ts`, `world.ts`) 
- **Created domain-based structure** with organized subdirectories:

#### `/api/agents/` - Agent Management Domain
- `crud.ts` - Create, read, update, delete operations
- `lifecycle.ts` - Start/stop/restart agent lifecycle management  
- `logs.ts` - Agent logging and diagnostics
- `panels.ts` - Agent panel/dashboard functionality
- `worlds.ts` - World/environment management for agents
- `index.ts` - Agent domain exports and routing

#### `/api/audio/` - Audio Processing Domain  
- `conversation.ts` - Conversational audio handling
- `processing.ts` - Audio processing utilities
- `synthesis.ts` - Text-to-speech and audio synthesis
- `index.ts` - Audio domain exports

#### `/api/media/` - Media Management Domain
- `agents.ts` - Agent-specific media operations
- `channels.ts` - Channel media management
- `index.ts` - Media domain exports

#### `/api/memory/` - Memory & State Management Domain
- `agents.ts` - Agent memory operations
- `groups.ts` - Group/collective memory management  
- `rooms.ts` - Room-based memory handling
- `index.ts` - Memory domain exports

#### `/api/messaging/` - Communication Domain
- `channels.ts` - Channel operations (moved from `messages.ts`)
- `core.ts` - Core messaging functionality
- `servers.ts` - Server-level messaging coordination
- `index.ts` - Messaging domain exports

#### `/api/runtime/` - Runtime Management Domain
- `debug.ts` - Runtime debugging utilities
- `health.ts` - Health checks and monitoring
- `logging.ts` - System-wide logging management
- `index.ts` - Runtime domain exports

#### `/api/shared/` - Common Utilities Domain
- `file-utils.ts` - File handling utilities
- `middleware.ts` - Security middleware (Helmet, rate limiting)
- `response-utils.ts` - Standardized API response helpers
- `validation.ts` - Input validation utilities
- `uploads/index.ts` - File upload handling
- `index.ts` - Shared utilities exports

#### `/api/system/` - System Configuration Domain
- `environment.ts` - Environment configuration (moved from `env.ts`)
- `index.ts` - System domain exports

#### `/api/tee/` - TEE (Trusted Execution Environment) Domain
- `tee.ts` - TEE operations (moved from `tee.ts`)
- `index.ts` - TEE domain exports

## Migration Details
### Before (prompts library):
```typescript
const response = await prompts({
  type: 'text', 
  name: 'value',
  message: 'Enter value:'
});
```

### After (@clack/prompts):
```typescript
const value = await clack.text({
  message: 'Enter value:',
  validate: (input) => input ? undefined : 'Value required'
});

if (clack.isCancel(value)) {
  clack.cancel('Operation cancelled.');
  process.exit(0);
}
```44:T87e,## Overview

In testing passing in calldata, we noticed that the `data` field was always `null` even though we were including it. When adding the changes to the `transferTemplate` to look for data, and also including it in the XML, we no longer saw it coming through as `null`. 

The template now properly extracts and passes through any provided data/calldata, and the extended type guard in `transfer.ts` addresses an edge case where data was sometimes sent as `'null'` which didn't trigger the initial default to `0x`.

## Changes

### Template Updates
Added support for data/calldata in the transfer template by adding:
- Instruction to extract "Additional data/calldata (if any is included)"
- XML response field `<data>string | null</data>`

Previously,  the `TransferParams` interface supported an optional data parameter but it seemed that not being included in the template meant it was always `null`, even when provided in the request.

These changes enable proper handling of transaction data in transfers while protecting against LLM response variations. The template now properly extracts and passes through any provided data/calldata, and the code safely normalizes edge cases to ensure valid transaction data.

The `bun.lock` change is from a fresh install of the repo.

**Note**: I tested this on version `1.0.6` but haven't been able to run `1.0.7` due to the character issue I filed on the main repo. Wanted to put up the PR in the meantime! Thank you -- greatly appreciate all the work ya'll are doing to transition!

### Type Guard Change to Handle Edge Case

Added additional defensive handling in `TransferAction` for LLM response edge cases:
```typescript
// Normalize empty or invalid data field to '0x'
if (!params.data || params.data === '0x') {
  params.data = '0x';
} else {
  // Handle case where template returns 'null' string
  params.data = (params.data as string) === 'null' ? '0x' : params.data;
}
```
This handles cases where the LLM returns 'null' as a string instead of a null value, ensuring the transaction data is always properly formatted.

The ElizaOS CLI had scattered and inconsistent directory detection logic throughout the codebase:

1. **Missing monorepo structure detection** - No proper classification for subdirectories within the ElizaOS monorepo
2. **Scattered detection functions** - Multiple functions like `isMonorepoContext()` duplicated detection logic across different files
3. **Infrastructure packages misclassified** - CLI, client, and other monorepo packages were incorrectly detected as `elizaos-project` instead of infrastructure components
4. **Inconsistent detection patterns** - Different commands used different approaches to determine directory types
5. **Over-aggressive heuristics** - Dependency-based detection caused false positives for infrastructure packages within the monorepo

## Solution

Implemented a centralized, hierarchical directory detection system with a new `elizaos-subdir` type:

### New Directory Type

- Added `elizaos-subdir` type for subdirectories within the ElizaOS monorepo that aren't projects or plugins

### Simplified Logic

- **Outside monorepo**: Check if project/plugin → if not, classify as `non-elizaos-dir`
- **Inside monorepo**: Check if project/plugin → if not, classify as `elizaos-subdir`

### Conservative Detection

- Made `isElizaOSProject()` more conservative inside monorepos by only using explicit indicators
- Disabled heuristic-based detection (dependency patterns, file structure analysis) within monorepos
- Consolidated duplicate detection functions

## Details

### Core Changes

**Directory Detection (`packages/cli/src/utils/directory-detection.ts`)**

- Added `elizaos-subdir` to `DirectoryInfo` type union
- Simplified main detection logic to use clear inside/outside monorepo branching
- Made `isElizaOSProject()` accept `monorepoRoot` parameter for context-aware detection
- Disabled heuristic checks inside monorepos to prevent infrastructure package misclassification

**CLI Commands**

- All CLI commands now use the standardized `detectDirectoryType()` function
- Removed deprecated `isMonorepoContext()` function usage

**Dependencies**

- Eliminated duplicate logic in `get-package-info.ts`
- Added `monorepoRoot` property to `DirectoryInfo` for consistent access
- Updated registry logic to use consolidated detection

### Test Results

Before fix:

```bash
# CLI directory incorrectly detected as project
✅ Directory Type: elizaos-project  # ❌ Wrong

# Client directory correctly detected
✅ Directory Type: elizaos-subdir   # ✅ Correct
```

After fix (expected):

```bash
# Both CLI and client correctly detected as subdirs
✅ Directory Type: elizaos-subdir   # ✅ Correct for both
```

### Directory Type Hierarchy

1. **elizaos-monorepo** - Root of ElizaOS monorepo
2. **elizaos-project** - User projects with agents/characters
3. **elizaos-plugin** - ElizaOS plugins
4. **elizaos-subdir** - Infrastructure packages within monorepo (CLI, client, core, etc.)
5. **non-elizaos-dir** - Regular directories outside ElizaOS context

## Problem

The ElizaOS CLI commands (`elizaos start` and `elizaos dev`) were creating database (`.elizadb`) and configuration (`.eliza`) files in incorrect locations, causing several issues:

1. **Monorepo Root Pollution**: When running from projects/plugins inside the monorepo, files were created in the monorepo root instead of the current working directory
2. **Subdirectory Execution Issues**: Running commands from subdirectories (e.g., `packages/cli`) would create files in the monorepo root, not the intended location
3. **Poor User Experience**: No clear guidance when users ran commands from inappropriate directories
4. **Inconsistent Behavior**: Different directory contexts resulted in unpredictable file placement

## Solution

Implemented comprehensive directory validation and centralized error handling:

### 1. **Centralized Directory Validation**

- Added `handleDirectoryContextErrors()` function for consistent validation across commands
- Integrated directory type detection into both `start` and `dev` commands
- Ensured database and config files are always created in the current working directory

### 2. **Enhanced User Guidance**

- **Fatal Errors**: Clear error messages for invalid directory contexts (e.g., running from subdirectories)
- **Warnings**: Styled warnings for monorepo usage with guidance toward best practices
- **Styled Messaging**: Consistent, color-coded error and warning displays matching existing UI patterns

### 3. **Improved Plugin Detection**

- Refined heuristics to reduce false positives in plugin detection
- Fixed issue where `project-tee-starter` was incorrectly flagged as a plugin

### 4. **Deferred Warning Display**

- Monorepo warnings now appear after agent initialization to avoid interrupting startup
- Uses global flag mechanism to schedule warnings for appropriate timing

## Examples

### Before (Problematic Behavior)

```bash
# Running from monorepo root
cd /path/to/eliza
elizaos start
# ❌ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza

# Running from plugin inside monorepo
cd /path/to/eliza/packages/plugin-bootstrap
elizaos start
# ❌ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza (monorepo root)

# Running from subdirectory
cd /path/to/eliza/packages/cli
elizaos start
# ❌ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza (monorepo root)
```

### After (Fixed Behavior)

```bash
# Running from monorepo root
cd /path/to/eliza
elizaos start
# ✅ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza
# ⚠️  Shows warning: "Running from monorepo root is not best practice"

# Running from plugin inside monorepo
cd /path/to/eliza/packages/plugin-bootstrap
elizaos start
# ✅ Creates .elizadb and .eliza in /path/to/eliza/packages/plugin-bootstrap
# ⚠️  Shows warning: "Running from monorepo root is not best practice"

# Running from subdirectory
cd /path/to/eliza/packages/cli
elizaos start
# ❌ Shows error: "Must run elizaos start from project or plugin directory"
# 🛑 Exits with helpful guidance to run "elizaos create"
```

This pull request introduces significant enhancements for handling real-time channel events, improving error feedback, and adding database operations for channel management. The changes include implementing a new `useChannelEvents` hook, extending the `SocketIOManager` class to handle channel-related events, and updating the database adapter to support channel updates and deletions.

### Real-time event handling:

https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge/issues/16

This most likely went unnoticed for three reasons:
1. Eliza still responds postively even if the memory is not stored as 'knowledge'.
2. Because the last few messages are in the context window, both the request to store the memory and the positive response from Eliza are in subsequent queries.
3. The browser client correctly calls `createUniqueUuid` with the agent runtime. 

# Risks
**Low Risk** - Instead of using `createUniqueUuid`, we are using `stringToUuid` in two different places

# Background
## What does this PR fix?
The way we generate unique ids for memories, specifically in `actions.ts`:

```ts
// for documents
clientDocumentId: createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + fileName + Date.now(), fileName),
// ...
// for text
clientDocumentId: createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now(), "user-knowledge"),
```

is not unique at all. for text, the result is always `4e394ea6-af35-0010-8047-94bccb8b8e5b` since `createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now(), "user-knowledge")` always results in `stringToUuid("user-knowledge:undefined")`. 

For documents, the result is `stringToUuid(fileName)` which is only unique if the file name is unique.

## Proposed Fix
To maintain the initial intent of the code, we should use `stringToUuid` for both text and documents using the existing seeds: `runtime.agentId + fileName + Date.now()` and `runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now() + "user-knowledge"`.

# Documentation changes needed?
No.

# Testing
## Where should a reviewer start?
1. Two new tests were added to `__tests__/action.test.ts` to ensure that the unique ids are generated correctly.
2. Existings tests were updated to Mock `Date.now()` to generate predictable ids. 
3. To truly verify knowledge is being stored, the "memories" table needs to be checked.

```typescript

    this.logger.debug('[registerDatabaseAdapter] Same adapter instance, skipping registration');

    this.logger.warn(

    return;
  }

## Changes
- **New `removeFromBunLock()` function**: Safely removes package entries from bun.lock using `bun remove`
- **New `executeInstallationWithFallback()` function**: Handles npm installation with automatic GitHub fallback and lockfile cleanup
- **Updated `installPlugin()` function**: Uses the new fallback mechanism for registry-based installations

## How it works

Plugin routes with URL parameters (`:id`, `:param`, etc.) returning 404 errors

# Risks

**Medium** - Changes to core plugin route matching logic in agent router
- Affects ALL plugins using parameterized routes
- Routing logic modification requires comprehensive testing

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes a fundamental routing issue where plugin routes with URL parameters were returning 404 errors. The agent router was using exact string matching instead of pattern matching, causing any route with parameters (`:id`, `:param`, etc.) to fail.

**Changes:**
- Added `path-to-regexp` import to agent router
- Replaced exact path matching (`path === r.path`) with pattern-based matching using `match()` function
- Properly extract URL parameters into `req.params` for plugin handlers

**Affects all plugins with routes like:**
- `/documents/:knowledgeId` (knowledge plugin)
- `/items/:itemId` (any plugin)
- `/users/:userId/settings` (any plugin)

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Review the changes in `eliza/packages/cli/src/server/api/agent.ts` - specifically the plugin route matching logic

## Detailed testing steps

**Test any plugin with parameterized routes:**
- Test knowledge plugin DELETE `/documents/:knowledgeId`
- Test any plugin GET routes with parameters like `/items/:id`
- Verify parameter extraction works correctly

**Before fix:**
```javascript
// path="/documents/12345", r.path="/documents/:knowledgeId"
if (path === r.path) // false - no match, 404 error
```

**After fix:**
```javascript
// Uses path-to-regexp matching for ANY plugin route
matcher = match("/documents/:knowledgeId")
matched = matcher("/documents/12345") // true - extracts parameters
req.params = { ...req.params, knowledgeId: "12345" }
```

**Files Changed:**
- `eliza/packages/cli/src/server/api/agent.ts` - Replace

**Problem:**
- Safari was failing to load the ElizaOS GUI on localhost HTTP in development
- The upgrade-insecure-requests CSP directive forced Safari to attempt HTTPS connections
- This caused SSL errors and blank screens when accessing localhost on any port in safari only

**Solution:**
- Implement environment-aware CSP configuration with different policies for dev vs prod
- Remove upgrade-insecure-requests directive in development for Safari compatibility
- Maintain full security (including HTTPS enforcement) in production environments

**Changes:**
- Extract isProd constant to eliminate repeated NODE_ENV checks
- Configure development CSP with useDefaults: false to disable upgrade-insecure-requests
- Configure production CSP with useDefaults: true to maintain security defaults
- Remove restrictive API router CSP that was overriding main app configuration
- Limit debug logging of environment details to non-production environments
- Add comprehensive comments explaining Safari compatibility requirements

**Security Impact:**
- Development: Maintains CSP protection without HTTPS enforcement (Safari compatible)
- Production: Full CSP security including HTTPS upgrade and HSTS headers unchanged
- No reduction in production security posture

**Testing:**
- Verified development mode removes upgrade-insecure-requests directive
- Confirmed production mode retains upgrade-insecure-requests directive
- Tested HSTS headers properly configured per environment
- Safari now successfully loads GUI on localhost in development

## What does this PR fix?
In `service.ts` There is a check to see if text content is base64 using an invalid regular expression. 

Old expression: `/^[A-Za-z0-9+/]+=*$/`

## Proposed Fix
1. Move base64 check to a utility function
2. New expression: `^(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{4})*(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{2}==|[A-Za-z0-9+/]{3}=)?$/`

See https://stackoverflow.com/questions/475074/regex-to-parse-or-validate-base64-data for details
GPT-4o also suggested the above expression.

No.

## Where should a reviewer start?

3. Since it's a small, self contained utility function, easy to try locally and add more use-cases. 

# Risks
**Low Risk** - This function is only used in one place and very easy to test.
